"b1b68f0d2a6d640c4b64c2bc98560b9e9a79a6a60b445899949b5db15b96aa90", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-WORK-003：工作负载分布：长度、复用、模态与相关性\n\n系统成本由输入输出的联合分布决定；平均长度会丢失影响缓存、批次和尾部行为的结构。\n\n## 不止两个平均数\n\nprompt 长度影响 prefill、KV 初始容量和传输；输出长度影响请求驻留时长、decode 工作和尾部；两者相关性决定长输入是否还占用很长输出时间。相同均值可以由全为中长请求，或极短与极长混合组成，调度结果很不一样。\n\n还应描述前缀共享长度与访问间隔、adapter 分布、grammar 类型、多样本分支、取消率和流式消费速度。视频帧数、分辨率、音频时长与 codec 设置形成额外几何，不能折算为文本字符数后丢失。\n\n## 复用有时间结构\n\n两条请求共享长前缀，只有在缓存尚未淘汰且被路由到可访问位置时才形成命中。把同一输入重复连续发送会得到近乎完美命中，与真实交错多租户的复用间隔不同。缓存容量、淘汰和亲和路由要在这个时间序列上一起分析。\n\n## 合成负载怎样保持问题结构\n\n可以用少量有含义的分层：短交互、长文档、长输出、共享系统提示、多模态与取消密集。每层保留长度范围、到达方式和质量目标，再按目标比例混合。这样能解释整体变化来自哪层，而不是只看一个总吞吐。\n\n长度边界也有实现意义：页长前后、窗口 rollover、图桶边界、最大上下文和 tokenizer 特殊 token。为机制验证构造边界输入，为容量验证保留真实分布，两类输入回答不同问题。\n\n## 输出长度是实现结果的一部分\n\n不同 EOS、stop string、采样或错误可能让优化版本更早结束，造成表面 throughput 提高。比较时应同时看质量、输入条件与实际输出长度；若任务允许不同长度，采用与业务目标对应的 goodput，而不是假设每输出 token 同等有用。\n\n## 为不同推理家族建立负载档案\n\n| 负载 | 至少保留的联合变量 | 容易误造的理想条件 |\n|---|---|---|\n| 多轮与 Agent 调用 | 回合依赖、历史增长、思考/工具时间、分支宽度 | 把依赖调用全部并行，或假设会话永不淘汰 |\n| RAG 与 reranking | 查询、候选数、文档长度、过滤与索引版本 | 只测热向量索引，不计取文档和重排 |\n| 离线批生成 | 批任务大小、期限、输入/输出联合长度、质量 | 只计算最快子集，忽略排空尾部 |\n| Embedding | 文本/图像长度、batch、向量维度、输出与归一化 | 固定极短输入却外推完整数据集 |\n| 推荐 | 稀疏特征数、表大小、热门度、重复 ID、dense 部分 | 所有 embedding 行都留在 cache |\n| 图推理 | 节点/边、度分布、邻居扩展、特征局部性 | 用均匀小图替代真实高阶节点 |\n\n这些变量不是额外的统一指标，它们决定不同执行图和资源需求。家族之间可能共享矩阵内核，却不能共用同一份输入分布假设或成功标准。\n\n## 保留相关性，再做有解释的分层\n\n长 prompt 与长输出同时出现时，会把输入计算和状态驻留压力叠加；热门 adapter 也可能与长前缀相关。独立抽样各变量会破坏这种结构。优先保留真实联合记录，再按长度、复用或模态进行分层；资料不足时明确哪些相关性是设定，做敏感性分析。\n\n取消并非总是均匀随机事件。客户端可能在等待过久、工具失败或用户改问后取消，所以取消率与延迟、历史长度相关。慢客户端的消费速度也会改变输出缓冲和 KV 驻留。把它们独立均匀化，可能低估真正的高压状态。\n\n## 开发、校准与保留集合\n\n机制边界集用来检查页长、桶边界和状态转换；代表分布集用来评估真实运行点；保留集检验反复搜索后的泛化。对量化，校准集还要覆盖敏感激活分布。三个用途可以来自同一总体，但必须记录划分和重用方式，不能看过保留结果后继续调参而仍称它独立。\n\n若只有一个 synthetic workload，结论应限定在其设定；变更长度长尾、复用间隔或到达方差后重新扫描，能够判断设计选择是否稳定。保存每层结果与总体加权结果，并解释权重来源，避免用有利的样本配比掩盖某类任务退化。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-GENAI-PERF：NVIDIA GenAI-Perf](../sources/S-GENAI-PERF.md)：GenAI-Perf 的指标围绕请求、响应、首个内容、流式间隔、输出长度和测量窗口定义。客户端 chunk 与模型 token 并非必然一一对应。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-GENAI-PERF/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-GENAI-PERF/fulltext.txt)\n\n- [S-PAGED：Efficient Memory Management for Large Language Model Serving with PagedAttention](../sources/S-PAGED.md)：PagedAttention 把每条序列的逻辑 KV 划分为固定 token 数的块，用映射表访问不连续物理存储。多个序列可以共享相同前缀的物理块，分叉写入需要保持各序列状态独立。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-PAGED/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-PAGED/fulltext.txt)\n\n- [S-QWEN-VL：Qwen2.5-VL Technical Report](../sources/S-QWEN-VL.md)：Qwen2.5-VL 报告讨论动态视觉分辨率、窗口视觉编码及时间相关的多模态位置，视觉输入具有独立的网格和时间几何。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-QWEN-VL/reader.html) · [全文文本](../../references/S-QWEN-VL/fulltext.txt)\n\n正文中的公式展开、容量算例与组合设计是基于这些来源的推导；设定算例不冒充实测。来源版本受 2026-04-30 上限约束，编辑日期 2026-10-22 不作为事实发布日期。此独立 base 版本内的章节修订号从 1 开始，原章节身份与版本映射保存在版本清单。\n\n## 延伸阅读\n\n- "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 2880]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 2880, "selected_characters": 2880, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "knowledge/models/embedding-reranking.md", "sha256": "44f22a27c9b4a11579bed108952f18722d90cd0d4c326db189c865d7e51aef38", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "related_chapter", "content": "# K-MODEL-013：Embedding 与重排：编码函数、批量与评分契约\n\nEmbedding 服务把一次输入变成固定维度表示，重排服务把一个查询与候选集合变成分数或顺序。它们的主要状态是模型、输入变换和中间张量，不能直接套用自回归 decode 的逐 token KV 生命周期。\n\n## 输出向量由整个编码函数定义\n\n完整链路是文本规范化与 tokenizer、特殊 token/segment/position、padding mask、编码器、pooling 或任务头、可选归一化、输出序列化。模型权重相同而 pooling、截断或 normalize 不同，会得到不同向量空间。索引侧与查询侧应遵循训练好的双塔契约；双塔权重可以共享也可以不同，但不能任意混配。\n\nBERT 固定论文描述双向 encoder：一个有效 token 在每层可看到同一输入中的左右文。句对作为一个序列输入，借助分隔符和 segment 区分；分类头读取特定聚合位置。它为 encoder 与句对评分提供结构依据，并不证明未经相应训练的 CLS 或任意平均池化天然适合语义检索。\n\n将同一文档从长度 128 截断到 64，会改变剩余 token 可见上下文，因而不只是丢掉后半部分输出。双向编码器通常不能像因果 decoder 那样把“前 64 个 token 的全部深层状态”作为未来追加内容的精确前缀缓存。可缓存的独立文档向量必须绑定完整有效输入与编码函数。\n\n## 双塔检索与交叉编码器重排\n\n双塔分别计算 e_q=f_q(q)、e_d=f_d(d)，文档向量可离线构建，查询只做一次编码并进行向量搜索。评分函数可以是内积、余弦或训练指定的其他形式。使用余弦时需要明确零范数处理；只有双方按同一规则归一化后，内积排序才与余弦一致。\n\n交叉编码器将 q、d 放入同一次 attention，并由任务头输出 s(q,d)。每个候选都要经过这条计算链，成本随候选数及句对长度增长，文档深层表示通常不能脱离查询独立复用。两者是不同质量/成本方案，不能把交叉编码器的分数当作普通向量内积接口。\n\n一个请求含 k 个候选时，系统可以将句对聚批，但必须保留查询 ID、候选 ID、原顺序及各自截断结果。分数归还错误的候选会表现成模型质量下降。若输出概率，须使用该任务头的校准与归一化定义；任意跨请求 softmax 会让同一候选的分数依赖一起排队的其他请求。\n\n## Padding、packing 与计算形状\n\n设一批有 B 个长度 nᵢ 的序列，隐藏维 d。投影和 MLP 的主要工作随 Σnᵢ 变化，密集 attention 的逻辑配对数随 Σnᵢ² 变化。统一 padding 到 nmax 后，相应工作变成 Bnmax 和 Bnmax²。按长度分组能减少浪费，但等待聚批增加在线延迟，并可能让长输入饥饿。\n\nPacking 把多个序列放入一个连续缓冲时，必须保留序列边界和各自合法的位置、segment 与 attention mask。仅拼接 token 后做一次全连接的双向 attention，会让不同请求相互影响。可用 ragged 或块对角可见性减少无效计算，前提是 backend 能表达该函数。普通 padding mask 也只屏蔽无效位置，不等于跨序列隔离。\n\nPooling 应按有效 token 集合计算。平均池化的分母、特殊 token 是否参与、最后有效位置如何确定，都属于模型制品。向量归一化的 epsilon 和输出 dtype 同样需要固定。取 tensor 的最后一列，在右 padding 的批中可能读到 padding；对左 padding 或位置重建也不能凭形状猜测其含义。\n\n## 驻留、缓存与阶段预算\n\n单次执行的主要资源为权重、输入、层间激活、attention 工作区和输出向量。融合 attention 可避免显式保存完整分数矩阵，但不能因此把算术工作当作线性。输出尺寸为 B d_out s；如果只需要 pooled embedding，尽早释放逐 token 隐藏状态比向宿主复制全部 token 表示更节省流量。\n\n文档向量缓存 key 至少包含编码器/任务头版本、tokenizer 与前处理、完整有效输入、截断与 pooling、normalize 和输出格式。重排分数缓存还依赖有序查询—文档对及评分头；候选序列整体归一化的评分还依赖集合本身。热点输入缓存与长度聚批之间有相互影响：命中后留下的冷请求长度分布可能改变，调优不能只看原始流量。\n\n端到端延迟由排队、分词/图像等输入处理、设备搬运、编码、pooling/评分、归还组成。小批短句可能由 tokenizer 或提交成本主导；长句 attention 与激活更重；大量文档离线建库则更重视吞吐、输出持久化和失败重试。三个负载不宜共用一个“每秒 token”指标。\n\n## C12-A：Embedding 与重排的完整验证场景\n\n设定一个已固定输入和 pooling 契约的 encoder，输出 768 维 FP32；在线批含四条 64 token 与四条 512 token 序列。统一 512 桶的 attention 配对数为 8×512²=2,097,152，按两类分批后为 4×64²+4×512²=1,064,960。约 1.97 倍只是逻辑配对数之比，不包含权重、投影、等待与 kernel 效率，不能直接作为时延加速。八个输出向量合计 8×768×4=24,576 字节。\n\n候选 A 使用统一桶，在有限等待窗口内组成一个批；候选 B 使用 64/512 两桶并分别设置最大等待时间。质量目标是保持每条输入的编码函数，服务目标是固定 offered workload 下的 p95/p99 响应和完成率。离线建库可放宽等待来提高利用率，在线稀疏到达时则可能选择 A 或更小批。\n\n先用单样本参考逐条计算，再对照两种批处理的完整向量、余弦/内积和候选排序。一个独立 pooling 单元算例取有效向量 (1,0)、(0,1)，padding 向量 (100,100)，规定均值只含前两行，结果应为 (0.5,0.5)，再 L2 归一化为 (1/√2,1/√2)。这是测试输入变换的设定算例，不代表任意 checkpoint 应采用均值池化。\n\n重排部分为每个查询固定 20 个候选，分别运行串行句对与聚批句对。先验证每个 score 与候选身份，再测 nDCG/MRR 或任务规定的相关性指标，不能用向量数值接近代替排序质量。覆盖空文本、只有特殊 token、超长截断、重复候选、同分排序、混合长度、取消一个子请求和部分失败。批量置换后还原顺序应保持语义。\n\n最后同时报告编码延迟、完整查询重排延迟、候选数分布、内存峰值与质量。若缩小候选数带来加速，需要把召回损失列为算法变化；若只改变聚批，则使用同一候选集合比较。这样才能区分系统调度收益和更少工作带来的表面收益。\n\n<!-- infraagents:provenance -->\n\n## 来源与推导\n\n- [S-BERT：BERT](../sources/S-BERT.md)：固定 v1 描述双向 Transformer encoder、token/segment/position 输入、CLS 句级任务头及句对/序列标注任务。\n  [完整原文](../../references/S-BERT/reader.html) · "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "references/S-CHAT/fulltext.txt", "sha256": "7e5e268a64c716d3e87ebb5c11989e16afab9f10589a2190e281a0800a255287", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "source_original", "content": "\n===== docs/source/en/chat_templating.md =====\n<!--Copyright 2024 The HuggingFace Team. All rights reserved.\n\nLicensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the \"License\"); you may not use this file except in compliance with\nthe License. You may obtain a copy of the License at\n\nhttp://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0\n\nUnless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software distributed under the License is distributed on\nan \"AS IS\" BASIS, WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied. See the License for the\nspecific language governing permissions and limitations under the License.\n\n⚠️ Note that this file is in Markdown but contain specific syntax for our doc-builder (similar to MDX) that may not be\nrendered properly in your Markdown viewer.\n\n-->\n\n# Chat templates\n\nThe [chat basics](./conversations) guide covers how to store chat histories and generate text from chat models using [`TextGenerationPipeline`].\n\nThis guide is intended for more advanced users, and covers the underlying classes and methods, as well as the key concepts for understanding what's actually going on when you chat with a model.\n\nThe critical insight needed to understand chat models is this: All causal LMs, whether chat-trained or not, continue a sequence of tokens. When causal LMs are trained, the training usually begins with \"pre-training\" on a huge corpus of text, which creates a \"base\" model.\nThese base models are then often \"fine-tuned\" for chat, which means training them on data that is formatted as a sequence of messages. The chat is still just a sequence of tokens, though! The list of `role` and `content` dictionaries that you pass\nto a chat model get converted to a token sequence, often with control tokens like `<|user|>` or `<|assistant|>` or `<|end_of_message|>`, which allow the model to see the chat structure.\nThere are many possible chat formats, and different models may use different formats or control tokens, even if they were fine-tuned from the same base model!\n\nDon't panic, though - you don't need to memorize every possible chat format in order to use chat models. Chat models come with **chat templates**, which indicate how they expect chats to be formatted.\nYou can access these with the [`apply_chat_template`] method. Let's see two examples. Both of these models are fine-tuned from the same `Mistral-7B` base model:\n\n<hfoptions id=\"template\">\n<hfoption id=\"Mistral\">\n\n```py\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer\n\ntokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\"mistralai/Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.1\")\nchat = [\n  {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"Hello, how are you?\"},\n  {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": \"I'm doing great. How can I help you today?\"},\n  {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"I'd like to show off how chat templating works!\"},\n]\n\ntokenizer.apply_chat_template(chat, tokenize=False)\n```\n\n```md\n<s>[INST] Hello, how are you? [/INST]I'm doing great. How can I help you today?</s> [INST] I'd like to show off how chat templating works! [/INST]\n```\n\n</hfoption>\n<hfoption id=\"Zephyr\">\n\n```py\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer\n\ntokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\"HuggingFaceH4/zephyr-7b-beta\")\nchat = [\n  {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"Hello, how are you?\"},\n  {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": \"I'm doing great. How can I help you today?\"},\n  {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"I'd like to show off how chat templating works!\"},\n]\n\ntokenizer.apply_chat_template(chat, tokenize=False)\n```\n\n```md\n<|user|>\\nHello, how are you?</s>\\n<|assistant|>\\nI'm doing great. How can I help you today?</s>\\n<|user|>\\nI'd like to show off how chat templating works!</s>\\n\n```\n\n</hfoption>\n</hfoptions>\n\nMistral-7B-Instruct uses `[INST]` and `[/INST]` tokens to indicate the start and end of user messages, while Zephyr-7B uses `<|user|>` and `<|assistant|>` tokens to indicate speaker roles. This is why chat templates are important - with the wrong control tokens, these models would have drastically worse performance.\n\n## Using `apply_chat_template`\n\nThe input to `apply_chat_template` should be structured as a list of dictionaries with `role` and `content` keys. The `role` key specifies the speaker, and the `content` key contains the message. The common roles are:\n\n- `user` for messages from the user\n- `assistant` for messages from the model\n- `system` for directives on how the model should act (usually placed at the beginning of the chat)\n\n[`apply_chat_template`] takes this list and returns a formatted sequence. Set `tokenize=True` if you want to tokenize the sequence.\n\n```py\nimport torch\nfrom transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer\n\ntokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\"HuggingFaceH4/zephyr-7b-beta\")\nmodel = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(\"HuggingFaceH4/zephyr-7b-beta\", device_map=\"auto\", dtype=torch.bfloat16)\n\nmessages = [\n    {\"role\": \"system\", \"content\": \"You are a friendly chatbot who always responds in the style of a pirate\",},\n    {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"How many helicopters can a human eat in one sitting?\"},\n ]\ntokenized_chat = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=True, add_generation_prompt=True, return_tensors=\"pt\")\nprint(tokenizer.decode(tokenized_chat[0]))\n```\n\n```md\n<|system|>\nYou are a friendly chatbot who always responds in the style of a pirate</s>\n<|user|>\nHow many helicopters can a human eat in one sitting?</s>\n<|assistant|>\n```\n\nPass the tokenized chat to [`~GenerationMixin.generate`] to generate a response.\n\n```py\noutputs = model.generate(tokenized_chat, max_new_tokens=128) \nprint(tokenizer.decode(outputs[0]))\n```\n\n```md\n<|system|>\nYou are a friendly chatbot who always responds in the style of a pirate</s>\n<|user|>\nHow many helicopters can a human eat in one sitting?</s>\n<|assistant|>\nMatey, I'm afraid I must inform ye that humans cannot eat helicopters. Helicopters are not food, they are flying machines. Food is meant to be eaten, like a hearty plate o' grog, a savory bowl o' stew, or a delicious loaf o' bread. But helicopters, they be for transportin' and movin' around, not for eatin'. So, I'd say none, me hearties. None at all.\n```\n\n> [!WARNING]\n> Some tokenizers add special `<bos>` and `<eos>` tokens. Chat templates should already include all the necessary special tokens, and adding additional special tokens is often incorrect or duplicated, hurting model performance. When you format text with `apply_chat_template(tokenize=False)`, make sure you set `add_special_tokens=False` if you tokenize later to avoid duplicating these tokens.\n> This isn't an issue if you use `apply_chat_template(tokenize=True)`, which means it's usually the safer option!\n\n### add_generation_prompt\n\nYou may have noticed the [add_generation_prompt](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/internal/tokenization_utils#transformers.PreTrainedTokenizerBase.apply_chat_template.add_generation_prompt) argument in the above examples.\nThis argument adds tokens to the end of the chat that indicate the start of an `assistant` response. Remember: Beneath all the chat abstractions, chat models are still just language models that continue a sequence of tokens!\nIf you include tokens that tell it that it's now in an `assistant` response, it will correctly write a response, but if you don't include these tokens, the model may get confused and do something strange, like **continuing** the user's message instead of replying to it!\n\nLet's see an example to understand what `add_generation_prompt` is actually doing. First, let's format a chat without `add_generation_prompt`:\n\n```py\ntokenized_chat = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=False)\ntokenized_chat\n```\n\n```md\n<|im_start|>user\nHi there!<|im_end|>\n<|im_start|>assistant\nNice to meet you!<|im_end|>\n<|im_start|>user\nCan I ask a question?<|im_end|>\n```\n\nNow, let's format the same chat with `add_generation_prompt=True`:\n\n```py\ntokenized_chat = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=True)\ntokenized_chat\n```\n\n```md\n<|im_start|>user\nHi there!<|im_end|>\n<|im_start|>assistant\nNice to meet you!<|im_end|>\n<|im_start|>user\nCan I ask a question?<|im_end|>\n<|im_start|>assistant\n\n```\n\nWhen `add_generation_prompt=True`, `<|im_start|>assistant` is added at the end to indicate the start of an `assistant` message. This lets the model know an `assistant` response is next.\n\nNot all models require generation prompts, and some models, like [Llama](./model_doc/llama), don't have any special tokens before the `assistant` response. In these cases, [add_generation_prompt](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/internal/tokenization_utils#transformers.PreTrainedTokenizerBase.apply_chat_template.add_generation_prompt) has no effect.\n\n### continue_final_message\n\nThe [continue_final_message](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/internal/tokenization_utils#transformers.PreTrainedTokenizerBase.apply_chat_template.continue_final_message) parameter controls whether the final message in the chat should be continued or not instead of starting a new one. It removes end of sequence tokens so that the model continues generation from the final message.\n\nThis is useful for “prefilling” a model response. In the example below, the model generates text that continues the JSON string rather than starting a new message. It can be very useful for improving the accuracy of instruction following when you know how to start its replies.\n\n```py\nchat = [\n    {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"Can you format the answer in JSON?\"},\n    {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": '{\"name\": \"'},\n]\n\nformatted_chat = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(chat, tokenize=True, return_dict=True, continue_final_message=True)\nmodel.generate(**formatted_chat)\n```\n\n> [!WARNING]\n> You shouldn't use [add_generation_prompt](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/internal/tokenization_utils#transformers.PreTrainedTokenizerBase.apply_chat_template.add_generation_prompt) and [continue_final_message](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/internal/tokenization_utils#transformers.PreTrainedTokenizerBase.apply_chat_template.continue_final_message) together. The former adds tokens that start a new message, while the latter removes end of sequence tokens. Using them together returns an error.\n\n[`TextGenerationPipeline`] sets [add_generation_prompt](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/internal/tokenization_utils#transformers.PreTrainedTokenizerBase.apply_chat_template.add_generation_prompt) to `True` by default to start a new message. However, if the final message in the chat has the `assistant` role, it assumes the message is a prefill and switches to `continue_final_message=True`. This is because most models don't support multiple consecutive assistant messages. To override this behavior, explicitly pass the [continue_final_message](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/internal/tokenization_utils#transformers.PreTrainedTokenizerBase.apply_chat_template.continue_final_message) argument to the pipeline.\n\n## Model training\n\nTraining a model with a chat template is a good way to ensure the template matches the tokens the model was trained on. Apply the chat template as a preprocessing step to your dataset. Set `add_generation_prompt=False` because the additional tokens to prompt an assistant response aren't helpful during training.\n\nAn example of preprocessing a dataset with a chat template is shown below.\n\n```py\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer\nfrom datasets import Dataset\n\ntokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(\"HuggingFaceH4/zephyr-7b-beta\")\n\nchat1 = [\n    {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"Which is bigger, the moon or the sun?\"},\n    {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": \"The sun.\"}\n]\nchat2 = [\n    {\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"Which is bigger, a virus or a bacterium?\"},\n    {\"role\": \"assistant\", \"content\": \"A bacterium.\"}\n]\n\ndataset = Dataset.from_dict({\"chat\": [chat1, chat2]})\ndataset = dataset.map(lambda x: {\"formatted_chat\": tokenizer.apply_chat_template(x[\"chat\"], tokenize=False, add_generation_prompt=False)})\nprint(dataset['formatted_chat'][0])\n```\n\n```md\n<|user|>\nWhich is bigger, the moon or the sun?</s>\n<|assistant|>\nThe sun.</s>\n```\n\nAfter this step, you can continue following the [training recipe](./tasks/language_modeling) for causal language models using the `formatted_chat` column.\n", "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 12274]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 12274, "selected_characters": 12274, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "references/S-HF-CACHE/fulltext.txt", "sha256": "373837b8ac246d43867eb21de168cc59e5e6e78f5f3b9c27bee54740c4a635b4", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "source_original", "content": "\n===== docs/source/en/cache_explanation.md =====\n<!--Copyright 2024 The HuggingFace Team. All rights reserved.\n\nLicensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the \"License\"); you may not use this file except in compliance with\nthe License. You may obtain a copy of the License at\n\nhttp://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0\n\nUnless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software distributed under the License is distributed on\nan \"AS IS\" BASIS, WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied. See the License for the\nspecific language governing permissions and limitations under the License.\n\n⚠️ Note that this file is in Markdown but contains specific syntax for our doc-builder (similar to MDX) that may not be\nrendered properly in your Markdown viewer.\n\n-->\n\n# Caching\n\nImagine you're having a conversation with someone, and instead of remembering what they previously said, they have to start from scratch every time you respond. This would be slow and inefficient, right?\n\nYou can extend this analogy to transformer models. Autoregressive model generation can be slow because it makes a prediction one token at a time. Each new prediction is dependent on all the previous context.\n\nTo predict the 1000th token, the model requires information from the previous 999 tokens. The information is represented as matrix multiplications across the token representations.\n\nTo predict the 1001th token, you need the same information from the previous 999 tokens in addition to any information from the 1000th token. This is a lot of matrix multiplications a model has to compute over and over for each token!\n\nA key-value (KV) cache eliminates this inefficiency by storing kv pairs derived from the attention layers of previously processed tokens. The stored kv pairs are retrieved from the cache and reused for subsequent tokens, avoiding the need to recompute.\n\n> [!WARNING]\n> Caching should only be used for **inference**. It may cause unexpected errors if it's enabled during training.\n\nTo better understand how and why caching works, let's take a closer look at the structure of the attention matrices.\n\n## Attention matrices\n\nThe **scaled dot-product attention** is calculated as shown below for a batch of size `b`, number of attention heads `h`, sequence length so far `T`, and dimension per attention head `d_head`.\n\n$$\n\\text{Attention}(Q, K, V) = \\text{softmax}\\left( \\frac{Q K^\\top}{\\sqrt{d_{\\text{head}}}} \\times \\text{mask} \\right) V\n$$\n\nThe query (`Q`), key (`K`), and value (`V`) matrices are projections from the input embeddings of shape `(b, h, T, d_head)`.\n\nFor causal attention, the mask prevents the model from attending to future tokens. Once a token is processed, its representation never changes with respect to future tokens, which means $ K_{\\text{past}} $ and $ V_{\\text{past}} $ can be cached and reused to compute the last token's representation.\n\n$$\n\\text{Attention}(q_t, [\\underbrace{k_1, k_2, \\dots, k_{t-1}}_{\\text{cached}}, k_{t}], [\\underbrace{v_1, v_2, \\dots, v_{t-1}}_{\\text{cached}}, v_{t}])\n$$\n\nAt inference time, you only need the last token's query to compute the representation $ x_t $ that predicts the next token $ t+1 $. At each step, the new key and value vectors are **stored** in the cache and **appended** to the past keys and values.\n\n$$\nK_{\\text{cache}} \\leftarrow \\text{concat}(K_{\\text{past}}, k_t), \\quad V_{\\text{cache}} \\leftarrow \\text{concat}(V_{\\text{past}}, v_t)\n$$\n\nAttention is calculated independently in each layer of the model, and caching is done on a per-layer basis.\n\nRefer to the table below to compare how caching improves efficiency.\n\n| without caching | with caching |\n|---|---|\n| for each step, recompute all previous `K` and `V`  | for each step, only compute current `K` and `V` |\n| attention cost per step is **quadratic** with sequence length | attention cost per step is **linear** with sequence length (memory grows linearly, but compute/token remains low) |\n\n## Cache class\n\nA basic KV cache interface takes a key and value tensor for the current token and returns the updated `K` and `V` tensors. This is internally managed by a model's `forward` method.\n\n```py\nnew_K, new_V = cache.update(k_t, v_t, layer_idx)\nattn_output = attn_layer_idx_fn(q_t, new_K, new_V)\n```\n\nWhen you use Transformers' [`Cache`] class, the self-attention module performs several critical steps to integrate past and present information.\n\n1. The attention module concatenates current kv pairs with past kv pairs stored in the cache. This creates attentions weights with the shape `(new_tokens_length, past_kv_length + new_tokens_length)`. The current and past kv pairs are essentially combined to compute the attention scores, ensuring a model is aware of previous context and the current input.\n\n2. When the `forward` method is called iteratively, it's crucial that the attention mask shape matches the combined length of the past and current kv pairs. The attention mask should have the shape `(batch_size, past_kv_length + new_tokens_length)`. This is typically handled internally in [`~GenerationMixin.generate`], but if you want to implement your own generation loop with [`Cache`], keep this in mind! The attention mask should hold the past and current token values.\n\n## Cache storage implementation\n\nCaches are structured as a list of layers, where each layer contains a key and value cache. The key and value caches are tensors with the shape `[batch_size, num_heads, seq_len, head_dim]`.\n\nLayers can be of different types (e.g. `DynamicLayer`, `StaticLayer`, `StaticSlidingWindowLayer`), which mostly changes how sequence length is handled and how the cache is updated.\n\nThe simplest is a `DynamicLayer` that grows as more tokens are processed. The sequence length dimension (`seq_len`) increases with each new token:\n\n```py\ncache.layers[idx].keys = torch.cat([cache.layers[idx].keys, key_states], dim=-2)\ncache.layers[idx].values = torch.cat([cache.layers[idx].values, value_states], dim=-2)\n```\n\nOther layer types like `StaticLayer` and `StaticSlidingWindowLayer` have a fixed sequence length that is set when the cache is created. This makes them compatible with `torch.compile`. In the case of `StaticSlidingWindowLayer`, existing tokens are shifted out of the cache when a new token is added.\n\nThe example below demonstrates how to create a generation loop with [`DynamicCache`]. As discussed, the attention mask is a concatenation of past and current token values.\n\n```py\nimport torch\nfrom transformers import AutoTokenizer, AutoModelForCausalLM, DynamicCache\nfrom accelerate import Accelerator\n\ndevice = Accelerator().device\n\nmodel_id = \"meta-llama/Llama-2-7b-chat-hf\"\nmodel = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(model_id, dtype=torch.bfloat16, device_map=device)\ntokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_id)\n\npast_key_values = DynamicCache(config=model.config)\nmessages = [{\"role\": \"user\", \"content\": \"Hello, what's your name.\"}]\ninputs = tokenizer.apply_chat_template(messages, add_generation_prompt=True, return_tensors=\"pt\", return_dict=True).to(model.device)\n\ngenerated_ids = inputs.input_ids\nmax_new_tokens = 10\n\nfor _ in range(max_new_tokens):\n    outputs = model(**inputs, past_key_values=past_key_values, use_cache=True)\n    # Greedily sample one next token\n    next_token_ids = outputs.logits[:, -1:].argmax(-1)\n    generated_ids = torch.cat([generated_ids, next_token_ids], dim=-1)\n    # Prepare inputs for the next generation step by leaving unprocessed tokens, in our case we have only one new token\n    # and expanding attn mask for the new token, as explained above\n    attention_mask = inputs[\"attention_mask\"]\n    attention_mask = torch.cat([attention_mask, attention_mask.new_ones((attention_mask.shape[0], 1))], dim=-1)\n    inputs = {\"input_ids\": next_token_ids, \"attention_mask\": attention_mask}\n\nprint(tokenizer.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0])\n\"[INST] Hello, what's your name. [/INST]  Hello! My name is LLaMA,\"\n```\n", "excerpt_ranges": [[0, 7977]], "range_unit": "zero-based half-open characters in extracted text", "extracted_characters": 7977, "selected_characters": 7977, "extraction": "UTF-8 text", "selection_reason": "full extracted text fits allocation"}, {"path": "references/S-ASGI/fulltext.txt", "sha256": "ba273c91f3e7b8c32f863e8b57145798c3b5c9275073f758a41e1cb0a6f5bee3", "reading_scope": "full", "category": "source_original", "content": "\n===== specs/www.rst =====\n====================================\nHTTP & WebSocket ASGI Message Format\n====================================\n\n**Version**: 2.5 (2024-06-05)\n\nThe HTTP+WebSocket ASGI sub-specification outlines how to transport HTTP/1.1,\nHTTP/2 and WebSocket connections within ASGI.\n\nIt is deliberately intended and designed to be a superset of the WSGI format\nand specifies how to translate between the two for the set of requests that\nare able to be handled by WSGI.\n\n\nSpec Versions\n-------------\n\nThis spec has had the following versions:\n\n* ``2.0``: The first version of the spec, released with ASGI 2.0\n* ``2.1``: Added the ``headers`` key to the WebSocket Accept response\n* ``2.2``: Allow ``None`` in the second item of ``server`` scope value.\n* ``2.3``: Added the ``reason`` key to the WebSocket close event.\n* ``2.4``: Calling ``send()`` on a closed connection should raise an error\n* ``2.5``: Added the ``reason`` key to the WebSocket disconnect event.\n\nSpec versions let you understand what the server you are using understands. If\na server tells you it only supports version ``2.0`` of this spec, then\nsending ``headers`` with a WebSocket Accept message is an error, for example.\n\nThey are separate from the HTTP version or the ASGI version.\n\n\nHTTP\n----\n\nThe HTTP format covers HTTP/1.0, HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2, as the changes in\nHTTP/2 are largely on the transport level. A protocol server should give\ndifferent scopes to different requests on the same HTTP/2 connection, and\ncorrectly multiplex the responses back to the same stream in which they came.\nThe HTTP version is available as a string in the scope.\n\nMultiple header fields with the same name are complex in HTTP. RFC 7230\nstates that for any header field that can appear multiple times, it is exactly\nequivalent to sending that header field only once with all the values joined by\ncommas.\n\nHowever, for HTTP cookies (``Cookie`` and ``Set-Cookie``) the allowed behaviour\ndoes not follow the above rule, and also varies slightly based on the HTTP\nprotocol version:\n\n* For the ``Set-Cookie`` header in HTTP/1.0, HTTP/1.1 and HTTP2.0, it may appear\n  repeatedly, but cannot be concatenated by commas (or anything else) into a\n  single header field.\n\n* For the ``Cookie`` header, in HTTP/1.0 and HTTP/1.1, RFC 7230 and RFC 6265\n  make it clear that the ``Cookie`` header must only be sent once by a\n  user-agent, and must be concatenated into a single octet string using the\n  two-octet delimiter of 0x3b, 0x20 (the ASCII string \"; \"). However in HTTP/2,\n  RFC 9113 states that ``Cookie`` headers MAY appear repeatedly, OR be\n  concatenated using the two-octet delimiter of 0x3b, 0x20\n  (the ASCII string \"; \").\n\nThe ASGI design decision is to transport both request and response headers as\nlists of 2-element ``[name, value]`` lists and preserve headers exactly as they\nwere provided.\n\nFor ASGI applications that support HTTP/2, care should be taken to handle the\nspecial case for ``Cookie`` noted above.\n\nThe HTTP protocol should be signified to ASGI applications with a ``type``\nvalue of ``http``.\n\n\nHTTP Connection Scope\n'''''''''''''''''''''\n\nHTTP connections have a single-request *connection scope* - that is, your\napplication will be called at the start of the request, and will last until\nthe end of that specific request, even if the underlying socket is still open\nand serving multiple requests.\n\nIf you hold a response open for long-polling or similar, the *connection scope*\nwill persist until the response closes from either the client or server side.\n\nThe *connection scope* information passed in ``scope`` contains:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"http\"``.\n\n* ``asgi[\"version\"]`` (*Unicode string*) -- Version of the ASGI spec.\n\n* ``asgi[\"spec_version\"]`` (*Unicode string*) -- Version of the ASGI\n  HTTP spec this server understands; for example: ``\"2.0\"``, ``\"2.1\"``, ``\"2.2\"``,\n  etc. Optional; if missing assume ``\"2.0\"``.\n\n* ``http_version`` (*Unicode string*) -- One of ``\"1.0\"``, ``\"1.1\"`` or ``\"2\"``.\n\n* ``method`` (*Unicode string*) -- The HTTP method name, uppercased.\n\n* ``scheme`` (*Unicode string*) -- URL scheme portion (likely ``\"http\"`` or\n  ``\"https\"``). Optional (but must not be empty); default is ``\"http\"``.\n\n* ``path`` (*Unicode string*) -- HTTP request target excluding any query\n  string, with percent-encoded sequences and UTF-8 byte sequences\n  decoded into characters.\n\n* ``raw_path`` (*byte string*) -- The original HTTP path component,\n  excluding any query string, unmodified from the bytes that were\n  received by the web server. Some web server implementations may\n  be unable to provide this. Optional; if missing defaults to ``None``.\n\n* ``query_string`` (*byte string*) -- URL portion after the ``?``,\n  percent-encoded.\n\n* ``root_path`` (*Unicode string*) -- The root path this application\n  is mounted at; same as ``SCRIPT_NAME`` in WSGI. Optional; if missing\n  defaults to ``\"\"``.\n\n* ``headers`` (*Iterable[[byte string, byte string]]*) -- An iterable of\n  ``[name, value]`` two-item iterables, where ``name`` is the header name, and\n  ``value`` is the header value. Order of header values must be preserved from\n  the original HTTP request; order of header names is not important. Duplicates\n  are possible and must be preserved in the message as received. Header names\n  should be lowercased, but it is not required; servers should preserve header case\n  on a best-effort basis. Pseudo headers (present in HTTP/2 and HTTP/3) must be\n  removed; if ``:authority`` is present its value must be added to the start of\n  the iterable with ``host`` as the header name or replace any existing host\n  header already present.\n\n* ``client`` (*Iterable[Unicode string, int]*) -- A two-item iterable\n  of ``[host, port]``, where ``host`` is the remote host's IPv4 or\n  IPv6 address, and ``port`` is the remote port as an\n  integer. Optional; if missing defaults to ``None``.\n\n* ``server`` (*Iterable[Unicode string, Optional[int]]*) -- Either a\n  two-item iterable of ``[host, port]``, where ``host`` is the\n  listening address for this server, and ``port`` is the integer\n  listening port, or ``[path, None]`` where ``path`` is that of the\n  unix socket. Optional; if missing defaults to ``None``.\n\n* ``state`` Optional(*dict[Unicode string, Any]*) -- A copy of the\n  namespace passed into the lifespan corresponding to this request. (See :doc:`lifespan`).\n  Optional; if missing the server does not support this feature.\n\nServers are responsible for handling inbound and outbound chunked transfer\nencodings. A request with a ``chunked`` encoded body should be automatically\nde-chunked by the server and presented to the application as plain body bytes;\na response that is given to the server with no ``Content-Length`` may be chunked\nas the server sees fit.\n\n\nRequest - ``receive`` event\n'''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent to the application to indicate an incoming request. Most of the request\ninformation is in the connection ``scope``; the body message serves as a way to\nstream large incoming HTTP bodies in chunks, and as a trigger to actually run\nrequest code (as you should not trigger on a connection opening alone).\n\nNote that if the request is being sent using ``Transfer-Encoding: chunked``,\nthe server is responsible for handling this encoding. The ``http.request``\nmessages should contain just the decoded contents of each chunk.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"http.request\"``.\n\n* ``body`` (*byte string*) -- Body of the request. Optional; if\n  missing defaults to ``b\"\"``. If ``more_body`` is set, treat as start\n  of body and concatenate on further chunks.\n\n* ``more_body`` (*bool*) -- Signifies if there is additional content\n  to come (as part of a Request message). If ``True``, the consuming\n  application should wait until it gets a chunk with this set to\n  ``False``. If ``False``, the request is complete and should be\n  processed. Optional; if missing defaults to ``False``.\n\n\nResponse Start - ``send`` event\n'''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent by the application to start sending a response to the client. Needs to be\nfollowed by at least one response content message.\n\nProtocol servers *need not* flush the data generated by this event to the\nsend buffer until the first *Response Body* event is processed.\nThis may give them more leeway to replace the response with an error response\nin case internal errors occur while handling the request.\n\nYou may send a ``Transfer-Encoding`` header in this message, but the server\nmust ignore it. Servers handle ``Transfer-Encoding`` themselves, and may opt\nto use ``Transfer-Encoding: chunked`` if the application presents a response\nthat has no ``Content-Length`` set.\n\nNote that this is not the same as ``Content-Encoding``, which the application\nstill controls, and which is the appropriate place to set ``gzip`` or other\ncompression flags.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"http.response.start\"``.\n\n* ``status`` (*int*) -- HTTP status code.\n\n* ``headers`` (*Iterable[[byte string, byte string]]*) -- An iterable\n  of ``[name, value]`` two-item iterables, where ``name`` is the\n  header name, and ``value`` is the header value. Order must be\n  preserved in the HTTP response.  Header names must be\n  lowercased. Optional; if missing defaults to an empty list. Pseudo\n  headers (present in HTTP/2 and HTTP/3) must not be present.\n\n* ``trailers`` (*bool*) -- Signifies if the application will send\n  trailers. If ``True``, the server must wait until it receives a\n  ``\"http.response.trailers\"`` message after the *Response Body* event.\n  Optional; if missing defaults to ``False``.\n\n\nResponse Body - ``send`` event\n''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nContinues sending a response to the client. Protocol servers must\nflush any data passed to them into the send buffer before returning from a\nsend call. If ``more_body`` is set to ``False``, and the server is not\nexpecting *Response Trailers* this will complete the response.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"http.response.body\"``.\n\n* ``body`` (*byte string*) -- HTTP body content. Concatenated onto any\n  previous ``body`` values sent in this connection scope. Optional; if\n  missing defaults to ``b\"\"``.\n\n* ``more_body`` (*bool*) -- Signifies if there is additional content\n  to come (as part of a *Response Body* message). If ``False``, and the\n  server is not expecting *Response Trailers* response will be taken as\n  complete and closed, and any further messages on the channel will be\n  ignored. Optional; if missing defaults to ``False``.\n\n\nDisconnected Client - ``send`` exception\n''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nIf ``send()`` is called on a closed connection the server should raise\na server-specific subclass of ``OSError``. This is not guaranteed, however,\nespecially on older ASGI server implementations (it was introduced in spec\nversion 2.4).\n\nApplications may catch this exception and do cleanup work before\nre-raising it or returning with no exception.\n\nServers must be prepared to catch this exception if they raised it and\nshould not log it as an error in their server logs.\n\n\nDisconnect - ``receive`` event\n''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent to the application if receive is called after a response has been\nsent or after the HTTP connection has been closed. This is mainly useful\nfor long-polling, where you may want to trigger cleanup code if the\nconnection closes early.\n\nOnce you have received this event, you should expect future calls to ``send()``\nto raise an exception, as described above. However, if you have highly\nconcurrent code, you may find calls to ``send()`` erroring slightly before you\nreceive this event.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"http.disconnect\"``.\n\n\nWebSocket\n---------\n\nWebSockets share some HTTP details - they have a path and headers - but also\nhave more state. Again, most of that state is in the ``scope``, which will live\nas long as the socket does.\n\nWebSocket protocol servers should handle PING/PONG messages themselves, and\nsend PING messages as necessary to ensure the connection is alive.\n\nWebSocket protocol servers should handle message fragmentation themselves,\nand deliver complete messages to the application.\n\nThe WebSocket protocol should be signified to ASGI applications with\na ``type`` value of ``websocket``.\n\n\nWebsocket Connection Scope\n''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nWebSocket connections' scope lives as long as the socket itself - if the\napplication dies the socket should be closed, and vice-versa.\n\nThe *connection scope* information passed in ``scope`` contains initial connection\nmetadata (mostly from the HTTP request line and headers):\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"websocket\"``.\n\n* ``asgi[\"version\"]`` (*Unicode string*) -- The version of the ASGI spec.\n\n* ``asgi[\"spec_version\"]`` (*Unicode string*) -- Version of the ASGI\n  HTTP spec this server understands; one of ``\"2.0\"``, ``\"2.1\"``, ``\"2.2\"`` or\n  ``\"2.3\"``. Optional; if missing assume ``\"2.0\"``.\n\n* ``http_version`` (*Unicode string*) -- One of ``\"1.1\"`` or\n  ``\"2\"``. Optional; if missing default is ``\"1.1\"``.\n\n* ``scheme`` (*Unicode string*) -- URL scheme portion (likely ``\"ws\"`` or\n  ``\"wss\"``). Optional (but must not be empty); default is ``\"ws\"``.\n\n* ``path`` (*Unicode string*) -- HTTP request target excluding any query\n  string, with percent-encoded sequences and UTF-8 byte sequences\n  decoded into characters.\n\n* ``raw_path`` (*byte string*) -- The original HTTP path component,\n  excluding any query string, unmodified from the bytes that were\n  received by the web server. Some web server implementations may\n  be unable to provide this. Optional; if missing defaults to ``None``.\n\n* ``query_string`` (*byte string*) -- URL portion after the\n  ``?``. Optional; if missing or ``None`` default is empty string.\n\n* ``root_path`` (*Unicode string*) -- The root path this application is\n  mounted at; same as ``SCRIPT_NAME`` in WSGI. Optional; if missing\n  defaults to empty string.\n\n* ``headers`` (*Iterable[[byte string, byte string]]*) -- An iterable of\n  ``[name, value]`` two-item iterables, where ``name`` is the header name and\n  ``value`` is the header value. Order should be preserved from the original\n  HTTP request; duplicates are possible and must be preserved in the message\n  as received. Header names should be lowercased, but it is not required;\n  servers should preserve header case on a best-effort basis.\n  Pseudo headers (present in HTTP/2 and HTTP/3) must be removed;\n  if ``:authority`` is present its value must be added to the\n  start of the iterable with ``host`` as the header name\n  or replace any existing host header already present.\n\n* ``client`` (*Iterable[Unicode string, int]*) -- A two-item iterable\n  of ``[host, port]``, where ``host`` is the remote host's IPv4 or\n  IPv6 address, and ``port`` is the remote port. Optional; if missing\n  defaults to ``None``.\n\n* ``server`` (*Iterable[Unicode string, Optional[int]]*) -- Either a\n  two-item iterable of ``[host, port]``, where ``host`` is the\n  listening address for this server, and ``port`` is the integer\n  listening port, or ``[path, None]`` where ``path`` is that of the\n  unix socket. Optional; if missing defaults to ``None``.\n\n* ``subprotocols`` (*Iterable[Unicode string]*) -- Subprotocols the\n  client advertised. Optional; if missing defaults to empty list.\n\n* ``state`` Optional(*dict[Unicode string, Any]*) -- A copy of the\n  namespace passed into the lifespan corresponding to this request. (See :doc:`lifespan`).\n  Optional; if missing the server does not support this feature.\n\n\nConnect - ``receive`` event\n'''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent to the application when the client initially opens a connection and is about\nto finish the WebSocket handshake.\n\nThis message must be responded to with either an *Accept* message\nor a *Close* message before the socket will pass ``websocket.receive``\nmessages. The protocol server must send this message\nduring the handshake phase of the WebSocket and not complete the handshake\nuntil it gets a reply, returning HTTP status code ``403`` if the connection is\ndenied.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"websocket.connect\"``.\n\n\nAccept - ``send`` event\n'''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent by the application when it wishes to accept an incoming connection.\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"websocket.accept\"``.\n\n* ``subprotocol`` (*Unicode string*) -- The subprotocol the server\n  wishes to accept. Optional; if missing defaults to ``None``.\n\n* ``headers`` (*Iterable[[byte string, byte string]]*) -- An iterable\n  of ``[name, value]`` two-item iterables, where ``name`` is the\n  header name, and ``value`` is the header value. Order must be\n  preserved in the HTTP response.  Header names must be\n  lowercased. Must not include a header named\n  ``sec-websocket-protocol``; use the ``subprotocol`` key\n  instead. Optional; if missing defaults to an empty list. *Added in\n  spec version 2.1*. Pseudo headers (present in HTTP/2 and HTTP/3)\n  must not be present.\n\n\nReceive - ``receive`` event\n'''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent to the application when a data message is received from the client.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"websocket.receive\"``.\n\n* ``bytes`` (*byte string*) -- The message content, if it was binary\n  mode, or ``None``. Optional; if missing, it is equivalent to\n  ``None``.\n\n* ``text`` (*Unicode string*) -- The message content, if it was text\n  mode, or ``None``. Optional; if missing, it is equivalent to\n  ``None``.\n\nExactly one of ``bytes`` or ``text`` must be non-``None``. One or both\nkeys may be present, however.\n\n\nSend - ``send`` event\n'''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent by the application to send a data message to the client.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"websocket.send\"``.\n\n* ``bytes`` (*byte string*) -- Binary message content, or ``None``.\n   Optional; if missing, it is equivalent to ``None``.\n\n* ``text`` (*Unicode string*) -- Text message content, or ``None``.\n   Optional; if missing, it is equivalent to ``None``.\n\nExactly one of ``bytes`` or ``text`` must be non-``None``. One or both\nkeys may be present, however.\n\n\n.. _disconnect-receive-event-ws:\n\nDisconnect - ``receive`` event\n''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent to the application when either connection to the client is lost, either from\nthe client closing the connection, the server closing the connection, or loss of the\nsocket.\n\nOnce you have received this event, you should expect future calls to ``send()``\nto raise an exception, as described below. However, if you have highly\nconcurrent code, you may find calls to ``send()`` erroring slightly before you\nreceive this event.\n\nKeys:\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"websocket.disconnect\"``\n\n* ``code`` (*int*) -- The WebSocket close code, as per the WebSocket spec. If no code\n  was received in the frame from the client, the server should set this to ``1005``\n  (the default value in the WebSocket specification).\n\n* ``reason`` (*Unicode string*) -- A reason given for the disconnect, can\n  be any string. Optional; if missing or ``None`` default is empty\n  string.\n\n\nDisconnected Client - ``send`` exception\n''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nIf ``send()`` is called on a closed connection the server should raise\na server-specific subclass of ``OSError``. This is not guaranteed, however,\nespecially on older ASGI server implementations (it was introduced in spec\nversion 2.4).\n\nApplications may catch this exception and do cleanup work before\nre-raising it or returning with no exception.\n\nServers must be prepared to catch this exception if they raised it and\nshould not log it as an error in their server logs.\n\n\nClose - ``send`` event\n''''''''''''''''''''''\n\nSent by the application to tell the server to close the connection.\n\nIf this is sent before the socket is accepted, the server\nmust close the connection with a HTTP 403 error code\n(Forbidden), and not complete the WebSocket handshake; this may present on some\nbrowsers as a different WebSocket error code (such as 1006, Abnormal Closure).\n\nIf this is sent after the socket is accepted, the server must close the socket\nwith the close code passed in the message (or 1000 if none is specified).\n\n* ``type`` (*Unicode string*) -- ``\"websocket.close\"``.\n\n* ``code`` (*int*) -- The WebSocket close code, as per the WebSocket\n  spec.  Optional; if missing defaults to ``1000``.\n\n* ``reason`` (*Unicode string*) -- A reason given for the closure, can\n  be any string. Optional; if missing or ``None`` default is empty\n  string.\n\n\nWSGI Compatibility\n------------------\n\nPart of the design of the HTTP portion of this spec is to make sure it\naligns well with the WSGI specification, to ensure easy adaptability\nbetween both specifications and the ability to keep using WSGI\napplications with ASGI servers.\n\nWSGI applications, being synchronous, must be run in a threadpool in order\nto be served, but otherwise their runtime maps onto the HTTP connection scope's\nlifetime.\n\nThere is an almost direct mapping for the various special keys in\nWSGI's ``environ`` variable to the ``http`` scope:\n\n* ``REQUEST_METHOD`` is the ``method``\n* ``SCRIPT_NAME`` is ``root_path``\n* ``PATH_INFO`` can be derived by stripping ``root_path`` from ``path``\n* ``QUERY_STRING`` is ``query_string``\n* ``CONTENT_TYPE`` can be extracted from ``headers``\n* ``CONTENT_LENGTH`` can be extracted from ``headers``\n* ``SERVER_NAME`` and ``SERVER_PORT`` are in ``server``\n* ``REMOTE_HOST``/``REMOTE_ADDR`` and ``REMOTE_PORT`` are in ``client``\n* ``SERVER_PROTOCOL`` is encoded in ``http_version``\n* ``wsgi.url_scheme`` is ``scheme``\n* ``wsgi.input`` is a ``StringIO`` based around the ``http.request`` messages\n* ``wsgi.errors`` is directed by the wrapper as needed\n\nThe ``start_response`` callable maps similarly to ``http.response.start``:\n\n* The ``status`` argument becomes ``status``, with the reason phrase dropped.\n* ``response_headers`` maps to ``headers``\n\nYielding content from the WSGI application maps to sending\n``http.response.body`` messages.\n\n\nWSGI encoding differences\n-------------------------\n\nThe WSGI specification (as defined in PEP 3333) specifies that all strings\nsent to or from the server must be of the ``str`` type but only contain\ncodepoints in the ISO-8859-1 (\"latin-1\") range. This was due to it originally\nbeing designed for Python 2 and its different set of string types.\n\nThe ASGI HTTP and WebSocket specifications instead specify each entry of the\n``scope`` dict as either a byte string or a Unicode string. HTTP, being an\nolder protocol, is sometimes imperfect at specifying encoding, so some\ndecisions of what is Unicode versus bytes may not be obvious.\n\n* ``path``: URLs can have both percent-encoded and UTF-8 encoded sections.\n  Because decoding these is often done by the underlying server (or sometimes\n  even proxies in the path), this is a Unicode string, fully decoded from both\n  UTF-8 encoding and percent encodings.\n\n* ``headers``: These are byte strings of the exact byte sequences sent by the\n  client/to be sent by the server. While modern HTTP standards say that headers\n  should be ASCII, older ones did not and allowed a wider range of characters.\n  Frameworks/applications should decode headers as they deem appropriate.\n\n* ``query_string``: Unlike the ``path``, this is not as subject to server\n  interference and so is presented as its raw byte string version,\n  percent-encoded.\n\n* ``root_path``: Unicode string to match ``path``.\n\n\nVersion History\n---------------\n\n\n* 2.0 (2017-11-28): Initial non-channel-layer based ASGI spec\n\n\nCopyright\n---------\n\n\nThis document has been placed
